## components/asset_pipeline_and_resources

| Id | Severity | File | Where | Finding | Proposed fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R3b-2 | High | architecture/components/asset_pipeline_and_resources.md | AST-002, AST-010, AST-116, AST-122, §2.1 `AssetCache`; `renderer/README.md` RND-001 and §2 `FrameRenderer::Create`; `platform_layer.md` PLT-038 steps 11 and 13; `ui_framework/README.md` UI-003; `animation_and_vfx/README.md` VFX-003 | No interface lets any component other than the renderer create or upload a GPU resource. Yet the asset cache creates textures and meshes and uploads them on the copy queue (AST-010, AST-116, AST-122). The UI framework owns its glyph-atlas pages (UI-003), and the VFX runtime owns the particle pool and shard buffers (VFX-003). RND-001 says the renderer owns the device and "every GPU resource of the client". The creation order is also circular: `FrameRenderer::Create` takes an `AssetCache&`, and PLT-038 mounts packs (step 11) before the renderer creates the device (step 13), so the asset cache has no device to create resources on. The first textured draw cannot be implemented without inventing this seam. | Declare in `renderer/README.md` §2 an engine interface owned by `FrameRenderer`, for example `GpuResourceFactory`. It creates a texture or buffer from a cooked description, schedules a copy-queue upload, returns its fence value, releases resources after the frames in flight, and supports re-registration on `DeviceRebuilt`. State its threading: creation from any thread, copy queue owned by the renderer. Give `AssetCache` a two-phase lifetime: mount and CPU loads after step 11, then `AttachGpu(GpuResourceFactory&)` after step 13. Amend RND-001 to "owns the device and the resource factory through which every client GPU resource is created", and have AST, UI (glyph pages) and VFX cite it. | open |
| R4a-6 | High | architecture/components/asset_pipeline_and_resources.md | §2.1: `TextureKind`, `ColorSpace`, `AlphaMode`, `MipPolicy`, `TintMarking`; against `data_schemas/import_and_build_records.md` §2 | [A] Five enumerations are declared in full in both documents, in `Engine`, with identical enumerators. But every cross-project include directory and output path is anchored on `$(SolutionDir)` (AGENTS.md §3, BLD-041, ADR-054). Built from `Tools\<Name>.slnx`, `$(SolutionDir)` is `Tools\`. `$(SolutionDir)<Project>` then names a folder that does not exist, and output lands in `Tools\x64\<Configuration>\`, which is exactly the latent breakage AGENTS.md §3 warns about. The tools solution cannot sit at the root either, because CI fails on two solutions there. As specified, the content build tool cannot be built. | This needs an owner decision, recorded as an amendment of ADR-054, and of AGENTS.md §3 if the owner agrees. Option A: anchor cross-project include directories and output paths on one repository-root property that each `.vcxproj` derives from its own location (for example `<SpeciesRoot>$(MSBuildThisFileDirectory)..\</SpeciesRoot>`), valid in both solutions, and have `Build\CheckProjectFiles.py` enforce it. Option B, if `$(SolutionDir)` stays: the tools solution links the static libraries that the game's solution built into `x64\<Configuration>\`, and CI builds the game's solution first. ## glossary

| Id | Severity | File | Where | Finding | Proposed fix | Status |

| R4b-48 | High | glossary/a_to_l.md | term "Collector" | "converts each into a Worker … outside the population cap". DEC-080 (O15) and R-ECON-073, R-ECON-074 make every delivery subject to the receiving team's cap: a Collector waits at its node while the team is population-locked. | "…at a friendly Spawn Node, subject to its team's population cap: it waits at the node while the team is at its cap (DEC-080)". | open |
| R3a-5 | Medium | glossary | term "host" | "host" (176 uses in 15 files) is undefined. The session lives in the host's process, the host fills empty slots with bots, closes slots, starts the match (R-MODE-010, R-META-087), and its loop rate bounds the tick rate (R-SIM-523). | Add a glossary row: "host: the participant whose process holds the hosted or private local session; it fills empty open slots with bot teams, may close slots and issues the match-start command (R-META-029, R-META-087, R-SIM-523)." Owner: reference/09 screen_flow_and_match_setup.md. | open |
| R3a-4 | Medium | glossary | term "participant" | "participant" is used 373 times across the reference documents ("at the same tick on every participant", R-MODE-069, R-SIM-530 to R-SIM-536, R-CTRL-014), but neither the glossary nor any document defines it. The design phase replaces the replicated reference model with server authority (O10, DEC-200), so the meaning of "on every participant" is exactly what it has to re-express. | Add a glossary row (engine term): "participant: a process that takes part in a match session and runs its own copy of the match world from the sequenced command frames, one tick at a time (R-SIM-530); the local participant is the one on this machine." Owner: reference/12 01_tick_timing_and_main_loop.md. | open |
| R3a-6 | Medium | glossary | term "session" | "session" (227 uses in 16 files) has no glossary row. R-META-029 defines the private local session and the hosted session in passing. | Add rows for "session", "private local session" and "hosted session", taken from R-META-029. Owner: reference/09 screen_flow_and_match_setup.md. | open |
| R3a-7 | Medium | glossary | terms "friend", "friendship" | "friend of" or "friend" is used 98 times in 29 files and decides ownership, damage filters and bot tests (R-MODE-302, R-BOT-047, R-SIM-414). It is defined by R-SIM-412 and R-SIM-413, but the glossary has no row for it: only "alliance" mentions "the friendship test". Requirements such as R-MODE-302 and R-BLDG-275 use the word without linking R-SIM-412. | Add a glossary row: "friend (friendship test): the relation of R-SIM-412 between two team ids, with the co-op group-0 rule of R-SIM-413; it decides actor-grid masks, damage filters, the Barrier Emitter contact rule and bot friend-or-foe tests (R-SIM-414)." Owner: reference/12 06_teams_and_player_commands.md. | open |

## reference/04_supply_crates_powers_and_weapons

| Id | Severity | File | Where | Finding | Proposed fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1b-1 | High | reference/04_supply_crates_powers_and_weapons/crates_and_powers.md | R-CRATE-102; §5 item 1; DEC-116 (decisions/verdicts_and_fixes.md); also R-SIM-550 (reference/12_simulation_model/01_tick_timing_and_main_loop.md) | Sample row 508 (fidelity, `wrong`). In the reference the Supply crate drop rule runs in slice 8 of every tick in which the match is not paused, and Supply crates drop during the grace period; the routing asked the library to state exactly that. The library states the opposite ("no Supply crate drops before the grace period is over"), and DEC-116 offers two options that both give no drops, so no decision records the change. The cause is probably R-SIM-550, which holds the whole world step while game speed is below the pause threshold, whereas the reference gates the world step only on its paused state. | Replace the last sentence of R-CRATE-102 with: "It has no grace-period gate: it runs in every tick in which the match is not paused, the ticks of the grace period included, so a Supply crate can drop before the grace period is over." Make §5 item 1 say the same. Re-open DEC-116 as Open: default A, the reference (drops during the grace period); option B, a grace-period gate. Then check R-SIM-550 and R-SIM-012 against the reference pause condition and state which world steps run during the grace countdown. | fixed (P6-RC) |

| R1b-2 | High | reference/10_audio_design/event_model_and_sound_data.md | R-AUD-021, table row "non-object event with an object or none"; architecture/components/audio_engine/instances_voices_and_envelopes.md AEN-130 | Sample row 366 (fidelity, `wrong`). When a non-object event is raised with no object, the reference instance starts at the world origin with no emitter, and its layer's position type still applies. The library says the instance has "no position and no emitter (a two-dimensional instance)". The two differ for a world-fixed (`fix`) layer, which the reference plays at the origin with distance attenuation. | Replace the cell with: "the object's position, with the object as the emitter; with no object, the world origin (0, 0, 0) and no emitter, and the layer's position applies: a `2d` layer plays at the listener, a `fix` layer at the origin, and an `att` layer is removed at the next audio update because it has no emitter (AEN-122 step 4, R-AUD-209)". Add the same no-object rule to AEN-130. If the two-dimensional form is wanted, record it as an Open DEC whose default is the origin. | fixed (P6-RD) |
